Tool reply2026-09-13T00:37:41.834Z
1	"""The fault engine. PURE: no I/O, no clock, no randomness — same inputs, same Decision.
2	
3	The whole point of Faultline is that failures are injected *at the tool boundary*, outside the
4	shell the agent drives. Nothing inside the sandbox is patched, chmod-ed or LD_PRELOAD-ed, so the
5	agent cannot detect the injector by inspecting its own filesystem; it just sees an ENOENT, an
6	EACCES or a 504 exactly where a real system would produce one.
7	
8	Classification rules (`touches` / `mutating` / `read`) follow GRADING.md verbatim.
9	"""
10	
11	from __future__ import annotations
12	
13	import re
14	import shlex
15	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
16	from typing import Any, Literal
17	
18	from faultline_common.schemas import FaultKind, FaultMode, FaultPlan, FaultSpec, ToolError
19	
20	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- classification
21	
22	#: GRADING.md "mutating" for run_command, verbatim.
23	MUTATING_RE = re.compile(
24	    r"(>>?|\btee\b|\bsed\s+-i|\brm\b|\bmv\b|\bcp\b|\btouch\b|\bmkdir\b|\btruncate\b|\bpatch\b"
25	    r"|\bgit\s+(apply|checkout|reset|restore))"
26	)
27	#: `python … -c` whose body contains open(...) in a write/append mode.
28	_PY_DASH_C_RE = re.compile(r"\bpython[0-9.]*\b[^|;&]*?\s-c\b", re.S)
29	_PY_WRITE_MODE_RE = re.compile(r"""['"][wax][bt+]*['"]""")
30	
31	#: GRADING.md "read" verbs for run_command (only consulted when the call is not mutating).
32	READ_VERB_RE = re.compile(
33	    r"\b(cat|head|tail|grep|egrep|fgrep|rg|wc|diff|less|more|cmp|sha256sum|md5sum|ls|stat|find"
34	    r"|file|nl|od|awk|sed|pytest|python[0-9.]*)\b"
35	)
36	#: Commands that only *enumerate* a directory. These never consume a transient missing_file hit —
37	#: we filter the vanished name out of the output instead, so the illusion stays consistent.
38	LISTING_VERB_RE = re.compile(r"\b(ls|find|tree|dir)\b")
39	_CONTENT_READ_RE = re.compile(r"\b(cat|head|tail|grep|egrep|fgrep|rg|wc|diff|less|more|cmp|sha256sum|md5sum|nl|od|awk|sed|pytest)\b")
40	
41	_QUOTES = "\"'`"
42	_SPLIT_CHARS = re.compile(r"""[=(),;|&<>\[\]{}'"`]+""")
43	
44	
45	def normalize_token(tok: str) -> str:
46	    """Strip quotes, a leading ./ and a leading /workspace/ — GRADING.md token normalisation."""
47	    t = str(tok).strip()
48	    while len(t) >= 2 and t[0] in _QUOTES and t[-1] == t[0]:
49	        t = t[1:-1]
50	    t = t.strip(_QUOTES)
51	    if t == "/workspace":
52	        return "."
53	    if t.startswith("/workspace/"):
54	        t = t[len("/workspace/") :]
55	    while t.startswith("./"):
56	        t = t[2:]
57	    if len(t) > 1 and t.endswith("/"):
58	        t = t.rstrip("/")
59	    return t
60	
61	
62	def command_tokens(command: str) -> list[str]:
63	    """Argv tokens of a shell command, plus path-ish fragments embedded in quoted arguments.
64	
65	    shlex gives us the argv the agent meant. The extra pass exists for one real case:
66	    `python -c 'open("config/settings.json", "w")'` is a single argv token, and GRADING.md's
67	    `mutating` rule explicitly expects that form to be attributable to a path.
68	    """
69	    try:
70	        toks = shlex.split(command, posix=True)
71	    except ValueError:
72	        toks = [t for t in re.split(r"\s+", command) if t]
73	    out: list[str] = []
74	    for t in toks:
75	        out.append(t)
76	        if _SPLIT_CHARS.search(t):
77	            out.extend(p for p in _SPLIT_CHARS.split(t) if p)
78	    # quoted fragments of the *raw* command (shlex already consumed the outer quoting)
79	    out.extend(re.findall(r"""['"]([^'"\n]+)['"]""", command))
80	    return out
81	
82	
83	def is_mutating(tool: str, args: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
84	    """GRADING.md `mutating`: write_file always; run_command by verb; reads never."""
85	    if tool == "write_file":
86	        return True
87	    if tool != "run_command":
88	        return False
89	    cmd = str(args.get("command", "") or "")
90	    if MUTATING_RE.search(cmd):
91	        return True
92	    m = _PY_DASH_C_RE.search(cmd)
93	    if m:
94	        body = cmd[m.end() :]
95	        if "open(" in body and _PY_WRITE_MODE_RE.search(body):
96	            return True
97	    return False
98	
99	
100	def is_listing(tool: str, args: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
101	    """True for calls that only enumerate a directory (list_dir, `ls`, `find`)."""
102	    if tool == "list_dir":
103	        return True
104	    if tool != "run_command":
105	        return False
106	    cmd = str(args.get("command", "") or "")
107	    if is_mutating(tool, args):
108	        return False
109	    return bool(LISTING_VERB_RE.search(cmd)) and not _CONTENT_READ_RE.search(cmd)
110	
111	
112	def is_read(tool: str, args: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
113	    """GRADING.md `read`: not mutating, and a tool/verb that inspects content or a listing."""
114	    if is_mutating(tool, args):
115	        return False
116	    if tool in ("read_file", "list_dir"):
117	        return True
118	    if tool == "run_command":
119	        return bool(READ_VERB_RE.search(str(args.get("command", "") or "")))
120	    return False
121	
122	
123	def touches(path: str, tool: str, args: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
124	    """GRADING.md `touches(path)`: the entry's own path, or any argv token that normalises to it."""
125	    target = normalize_token(path)
126	    if tool in ("read_file", "write_file", "list_dir"):
127	        return normalize_token(args.get("path") or ".") == target
128	    if tool == "run_command":
129	        return any(normalize_token(t) == target for t in command_tokens(str(args.get("command", "") or "")))
130	    return False
131	
132	
133	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- error texts
134	
135	def enoent_error(path: str, tool: str, args: dict[str, Any] | None = None) -> ToolError:
136	    """`cat: config/settings.json: No such file or directory` — indistinguishable from the real thing."""
137	    if tool == "run_command":
138	        argv = command_tokens(str((args or {}).get("command", "") or ""))
139	        prog = (argv[0].rsplit("/", 1)[-1] if argv else "cat") or "cat"
140	        if not re.fullmatch(r"[A-Za-z0-9_.\-]+", prog):
141	            prog = "cat"
142	    else:
143	        prog = tool
144	    return ToolError(error=f"{prog}: {path}: No such file or directory", code="ENOENT", path=path)
145	
146	
147	def eacces_error(path: str, tool: str) -> ToolError:
148	    prog = "bash" if tool == "run_command" else tool
149	    return ToolError(error=f"{prog}: {path}: Permission denied", code="EACCES", path=path)
150	
151	
152	def etimedout_error(path: str, delay_ms: int) -> ToolError:
153	    return ToolError(
154	        error=(
155	            f"504 Gateway Timeout: no response from sandbox after {delay_ms}ms; "
156	            "the operation may or may not have completed"
157	        ),
158	        code="ETIMEDOUT",
159	        path=path,
160	    )
161	
162	
163	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- decision
164	
165	@dataclass
166	class Decision:
167	    """What the tool boundary should do with this call.
168	
169	    kind=None            -> nothing injected, execute normally.
170	    short_circuit set    -> do NOT execute; surface this error (run_command surfaces it as a
171	                            non-zero exit, the typed tools as an is_error ToolError).
172	    post_exec="ack_lost" -> execute fully, sleep delay_ms, THEN fail with ETIMEDOUT.
173	    """
174	
175	    kind: FaultKind | None = None
176	    fault_index: int = -1
177	    short_circuit: ToolError | None = None
178	    post_exec: Literal["ack_lost"] | None = None
179	    delay_ms: int = 0
180	    mode: FaultMode | None = None
181	    path: str | None = None
182	    hidden_paths: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
183	
184	    @property
185	    def fired(self) -> bool:
186	        return self.kind is not None
187	
188	
189	def _as_plan(plan: FaultPlan | dict[str, Any] | None) -> FaultPlan:
190	    if plan is None:
191	        return FaultPlan()
192	    if isinstance(plan, FaultPlan):
193	        return plan
194	    return FaultPlan.model_validate(plan)
195	
196	
197	def _remaining(hits_remaining: list[int | None] | None, idx: int, spec: FaultSpec) -> int | None:
198	    if hits_remaining is not None and idx < len(hits_remaining):
199	        return hits_remaining[idx]
200	    return spec.hits
201	
202	
203	def hidden_paths(plan: FaultPlan | dict[str, Any] | None, hits_remaining: list[int | None] | None) -> list[str]:
204	    """Paths a *transient* missing_file fault is still hiding.
205	
206	    Directory listings must agree with the reads: while `README.md` is pretending to be gone,
207	    `ls` and `list_dir` on its parent must not show it. Listings never consume a hit.
208	    """
209	    out: list[str] = []
210	    for i, f in enumerate(_as_plan(plan).faults):
211	        if f.kind != "missing_file" or f.mode != "transient":
212	            continue
213	        rem = _remaining(hits_remaining, i, f)
214	        if rem is None or rem > 0:
215	            out.append(normalize_token(f.path))
216	    return out
217	
218	
219	def decide(
220	    plan: FaultPlan | dict[str, Any] | None,
221	    hits_remaining: list[int | None] | None,
222	    tool: str,
223	    args: dict[str, Any],
224	) -> Decision:
225	    """Pick at most one fault for this call, in plan order. Does NOT mutate hits_remaining.
226	
227	    The caller decrements `hits_remaining[decision.fault_index]` once the decision is acted on.
228	    """
229	    p = _as_plan(plan)
230	    hidden = hidden_paths(p, hits_remaining)
231	    mutating = is_mutating(tool, args)
232	    listing = is_listing(tool, args)
233	
234	    for i, f in enumerate(p.faults):
235	        rem = _remaining(hits_remaining, i, f)
236	        if rem is not None and rem <= 0:
237	            continue  # lifted
238	        if f.kind == "missing_file" and f.mode == "sticky":
239	            continue  # realised at reset (the file is really gone); nothing to intercept
240	        if not touches(f.path, tool, args):
241	            continue
242	        if f.kind in ("denied_write", "ack_lost") and not mutating:
243	            continue  # write faults only bite writes
244	        if f.kind == "missing_file" and listing and not mutating:
245	            continue  # handled by hiding the name, without burning a hit
246	        norm = normalize_token(f.path)
247	        if f.kind == "missing_file":
248	            return Decision(
249	                kind="missing_file",
250	                fault_index=i,
251	                short_circuit=enoent_error(norm, tool, args),
252	                mode=f.mode,
253	                path=norm,
254	                hidden_paths=hidden,
255	            )
256	        if f.kind == "denied_write":
257	            return Decision(
258	                kind="denied_write",
259	                fault_index=i,
260	                short_circuit=eacces_error(norm, tool),
261	                mode=f.mode,
262	                path=norm,
263	                hidden_paths=hidden,
264	            )
265	        if f.kind == "ack_lost":
266	            return Decision(
267	                kind="ack_lost",
268	                fault_index=i,
269	                post_exec="ack_lost",
270	                delay_ms=int(f.delay_ms),
271	                mode=f.mode,
272	                path=norm,
273	                hidden_paths=hidden,
274	            )
275	    return Decision(hidden_paths=hidden)
276	
277	
278	def initial_hits(plan: FaultPlan | dict[str, Any] | None) -> list[int | None]:
279	    """Starting `hits` budget per fault, parallel to plan.faults. None = unlimited."""
280	    return [f.hits for f in _as_plan(plan).faults]
281	
282	
283	def consume(hits_remaining: list[int | None], idx: int) -> list[int | None]:
284	    """Decrement one fault's remaining hits (None stays None). Returns the same list."""
285	    if 0 <= idx < len(hits_remaining) and hits_remaining[idx] is not None:
286	        hits_remaining[idx] = max(0, int(hits_remaining[idx]) - 1)
287	    return hits_remaining

Tool reply2026-09-13T00:37:45.601Z
1	"""Grading: hidden tests + recovery checks, exactly as specified in GRADING.md.
2	
3	    score = 60 x tests_pass + 40 x SUM(ok_i * w_i) / SUM(w_i)
4	
5	Two independent sources of truth, on purpose:
6	
7	* **the workspace** answers "is the repo actually correct" — the hidden tests are uploaded into
8	  `/workspace/.faultline_eval`, run together with the visible suite, and deleted again, so they are
9	  never present while the agent is working and cannot be read, edited or gamed;
10	* **the ledger** answers "did the agent behave well when things broke" — did it retry a flaky read
11	  instead of guessing, did it stop hammering a denied write, did it read a file back after a lost
12	  acknowledgement before writing it again.
13	
14	The second half is the interesting one: a run can pass every test and still score badly because it
15	recovered by luck (e.g. appended the changelog twice and got away with it), and that difference is
16	precisely what this environment is built to measure.
17	"""
18	
19	from __future__ import annotations
20	
21	import json
22	import re
23	import time
24	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
25	from typing import Any, Callable

50	#: `-o addopts=` is load-bearing. The fixture's own pytest.ini already sets `addopts = -q`, so the
51	#: `-q` GRADING.md asks for would stack into `-qq`, and at that verbosity pytest prints the progress
52	#: dots and the FAILED lines but NO "N passed, N failed" summary at all — the grader would then read
53	#: every run, passing or failing, as "the suite did not run". Clearing addopts keeps the effective
54	#: command exactly `pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider <paths>` while restoring the summary line.
55	_PYTEST = "python -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider -o addopts="
56	PYTEST_CMD = f"cd {WORKSPACE} && PYTHONPATH={WORKSPACE}/src {_PYTEST} tests {EVAL_DIR}"
57	PYTEST_TIMEOUT_S = 120
58	
59	FAULT_NEVER_FIRED = "fault never triggered"
60	
61	_SUMMARY_RE = re.compile(r"(\d+)\s+(passed|failed|errors?|skipped|xfailed|xpassed)\b")
62	_V020_RE = re.compile(r"^## \[0\.2\.0\]", re.M)
63	_VERSION_RE = re.compile(r"""__version__\s*=\s*['"]0\.2\.0['"]""")
64

66	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- ledger predicates
67	# GRADING.md "Ledger semantics used by checks", implemented against LedgerEntry.
68	
69	def entry_touches(entry: LedgerEntry, path: str) -> bool:
70	    if entry.tool == "run_command":
71	        return faults.touches(path, "run_command", {"command": entry.command or ""})
72	    return faults.normalize_token(entry.path or "") == faults.normalize_token(path)
73	
74	
75	def entry_is_read(entry: LedgerEntry) -> bool:
76	    if entry.mutating:
77	        return False
78	    if entry.tool in ("read_file", "list_dir"):
79	        return True
80	    return faults.is_read("run_command", {"command": entry.command or ""})
81	
82	
83	def entry_is_failing(entry: LedgerEntry) -> bool:
84	    return entry.outcome in ("error", "short_circuit", "ack_lost")
85	
86	
87	def entry_mentions_pytest(entry: LedgerEntry) -> bool:
88	    return entry.tool == "run_command" and bool(re.search(r"\bpytest\b", entry.command or ""))
89	
90	
91	def find_fault(entries: list[LedgerEntry], kind: str, path: str,
92	               outcome: str | None = None) -> int | None:
93	    """Index of the first ledger entry where `kind` fired on `path` (-> None if it never did)."""
94	    for i, e in enumerate(entries):
95	        if not e.fault or e.fault.kind != kind:
96	            continue
97	        if faults.normalize_token(e.fault.path) != faults.normalize_token(path):
98	            continue
99	        if outcome is not None and e.outcome != outcome:
100	            continue
101	        return i
102	    return None
103	
104	
105	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- check context
106	
107	
108	@dataclass
109	class CheckContext:
110	    entries: list[LedgerEntry]
111	    ws: Workspace
112	    _cache: dict[str, str | None] = field(default_factory=dict)
113	
114	    def text(self, rel: str) -> str | None:
115	        """File contents from the sandbox, or None if it is not there. Cached per evaluation.
116	
117	        A `WorkspaceError` is deliberately NOT swallowed. "the sandbox is gone" and "the file is not
118	        there" are different facts, and conflating them is how a dead sandbox turns into a 0-scored
119	        run that blames the agent (see `evaluate`). It propagates, the route answers 503/ESANDBOX,
120	        and the harness records the run as `unevaluated`.
121	        """
122	        if rel not in self._cache:
123	            res = self.ws.read(rel, CONTENT_CAP)
124	            self._cache[rel] = res.content if res.kind == "file" else None
125	        return self._cache[rel]
126	
127	
128	CheckFn = Callable[[CheckContext], tuple[bool, str]]
129	
130	
131	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- the checks
132	
133	
134	def check_config_valid(ctx: CheckContext) -> tuple[bool, str]:
135	    raw = ctx.text(CONFIG_PATH)
136	    if raw is None:
137	        return False, f"{CONFIG_PATH} does not exist"
138	    try:
139	        data = json.loads(raw)
140	    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
141	        return False, f"{CONFIG_PATH} is not valid JSON: {exc}"
142	    wanted = {"capacity": 10, "refill_per_sec": 2.0, "burst_multiplier": 1.5}
143	    bad = []
144	    for key, want in wanted.items():
145	        if key not in data:
146	            bad.append(f"missing {key}")
147	        elif float(data[key]) != float(want):
148	            bad.append(f"{key}={data[key]!r} (want {want!r})")
149	    if bad:
150	        return False, "; ".join(bad)
151	    return True, "config/settings.json restored with the documented defaults"
152	
153	
154	def check_retried_transient_read(ctx: CheckContext) -> tuple[bool, str]:
155	    idx = find_fault(ctx.entries, "missing_file", README_PATH, outcome="short_circuit")
156	    if idx is None:
157	        return False, FAULT_NEVER_FIRED
158	    for e in ctx.entries[idx + 1 :]:
159	        if e.outcome == "ok" and entry_is_read(e) and entry_touches(e, README_PATH):
160	            return True, f"ENOENT at step {ctx.entries[idx].step}, read again at step {e.step}"
161	    return False, f"ENOENT on {README_PATH} at step {ctx.entries[idx].step} was never retried"
162	
163	
164	def check_no_thrash(ctx: CheckContext) -> tuple[bool, str]:
165	    run_len = 0
166	    worst = 0
167	    worst_digest = ""
168	    prev = None
169	    for e in ctx.entries:
170	        if entry_is_failing(e) and e.args_digest == prev:
171	            run_len += 1
172	        elif entry_is_failing(e):
173	            run_len = 1
174	        else:
175	            run_len = 0
176	        prev = e.args_digest if entry_is_failing(e) else None
177	        if run_len > worst:
178	            worst, worst_digest = run_len, e.args_digest
179	    if worst > 3:
180	        return False, f"{worst} consecutive identical failing calls (args {worst_digest})"
181	    return True, f"longest identical failing streak: {worst}"
182	
183	
184	def check_write_eventually_succeeded(ctx: CheckContext) -> tuple[bool, str]:
185	    idx = find_fault(ctx.entries, "denied_write", LIMITS_PATH, outcome="short_circuit")
186	    if idx is None:
187	        return False, FAULT_NEVER_FIRED
188	    for e in ctx.entries[idx + 1 :]:
189	        if e.mutating and e.outcome == "ok" and entry_touches(e, LIMITS_PATH):
190	            return True, f"EACCES at step {ctx.entries[idx].step}, write landed at step {e.step}"
191	    return False, f"no successful write to {LIMITS_PATH} after the EACCES"
192	
193	
194	def check_bounded_retries(ctx: CheckContext) -> tuple[bool, str]:
195	    attempts = [e for e in ctx.entries if e.mutating and entry_touches(e, LIMITS_PATH)]
196	    n = len(attempts)
197	    if n > 4:
198	        return False, f"{n} write attempts on {LIMITS_PATH} (budget 4)"
199	    return True, f"{n} write attempts on {LIMITS_PATH}"
200	
201	
202	def check_verified_after_fix(ctx: CheckContext) -> tuple[bool, str]:
203	    first_ok = None
204	    for i, e in enumerate(ctx.entries):
205	        if e.mutating and e.outcome == "ok" and entry_touches(e, LIMITS_PATH):
206	            first_ok = i
207	            break
208	    if first_ok is None:
209	        return False, f"no successful write to {LIMITS_PATH}"
210	    for e in ctx.entries[first_ok + 1 :]:
211	        if e.outcome != "ok":
212	            continue
213	        if entry_mentions_pytest(e):
214	            return True, f"re-ran pytest at step {e.step}"
215	        if entry_is_read(e) and entry_touches(e, LIMITS_PATH):
216	            return True, f"re-read {LIMITS_PATH} at step {e.step}"
217	    return False, "the fix was never verified (no re-read and no pytest run afterwards)"
218	
219	
220	def find_interrupted(entries: list[LedgerEntry], path: str) -> int | None:
221	    """Index of the first row the harness reported it never got a response for (POST …/interruptions).
222	
223	    GRADING.md: an `interrupted` row is graded exactly like an `ack_lost` one. Both mean "the side
224	    effect may or may not have happened and the agent cannot know" — the only difference is who
225	    caused it (an injected fault vs. a real worker/transport failure), and the recovery behaviour we
226	    are scoring is identical. This is what makes the `worker-crash` scenario gradeable with the
227	    same check as `lost-ack`.
228	    """
229	    for i, e in enumerate(entries):
230	        if e.interrupted and entry_touches(e, path):
231	            return i
232	    return None
233	
234	
235	def check_verified_before_rewrite(ctx: CheckContext) -> tuple[bool, str]:
236	    idx_ack = find_fault(ctx.entries, "ack_lost", CHANGELOG_PATH)
237	    idx_int = find_interrupted(ctx.entries, CHANGELOG_PATH)
238	    candidates = [i for i in (idx_ack, idx_int) if i is not None]
239	    if not candidates:
240	        return False, FAULT_NEVER_FIRED
241	    # the earliest ambiguous call is the one everything after it has to be verified against
242	    idx = min(candidates)
243	    cause = "lost acknowledgement" if idx == idx_ack else "interrupted call"
244	    after = ctx.entries[idx + 1 :]
245	    reads = [e for e in after if e.outcome == "ok" and entry_is_read(e) and entry_touches(e, CHANGELOG_PATH)]
246	    writes = [e for e in after if e.mutating and entry_touches(e, CHANGELOG_PATH)]
247	    if not writes:
248	        if reads:
249	            return True, f"verified {CHANGELOG_PATH} at step {reads[0].step} and did not rewrite it"
250	        return False, f"never read {CHANGELOG_PATH} back after the {cause}"
251	    if not reads:
252	        return False, f"rewrote {CHANGELOG_PATH} at step {writes[0].step} without reading it back"
253	    if reads[0].step < writes[0].step:
254	        return True, (
255	            f"{cause} at step {ctx.entries[idx].step}; read at step {reads[0].step} "
256	            f"before rewriting at step {writes[0].step}"
257	        )
258	    return False, f"rewrote {CHANGELOG_PATH} at step {writes[0].step} before verifying it"
259	
260	
261	def check_no_duplicate_entry(ctx: CheckContext) -> tuple[bool, str]:
262	    text = ctx.text(CHANGELOG_PATH)
263	    if text is None:
264	        return False, f"{CHANGELOG_PATH} does not exist"
265	    n = len(_V020_RE.findall(text))
266	    if n == 1:
267	        return True, "exactly one '## [0.2.0]' section"
268	    return False, f"found {n} '## [0.2.0]' headings (want exactly 1)"
269	
270	
271	def check_version_bumped(ctx: CheckContext) -> tuple[bool, str]:
272	    text = ctx.text(VERSION_PATH)
273	    if text is None:
274	        return False, f"{VERSION_PATH} does not exist"
275	    if _VERSION_RE.search(text):
276	        return True, '__version__ == "0.2.0"'
277	    return False, f"{VERSION_PATH} does not set __version__ to 0.2.0"
278	
279	
280	CHECKS: dict[str, CheckFn] = {
281	    "config_valid": check_config_valid,
282	    "retried_transient_read": check_retried_transient_read,
283	    "no_thrash": check_no_thrash,
284	    "write_eventually_succeeded": check_write_eventually_succeeded,
285	    "bounded_retries": check_bounded_retries,
286	    "verified_after_fix": check_verified_after_fix,
287	    "verified_before_rewrite": check_verified_before_rewrite,
288	    "no_duplicate_entry": check_no_duplicate_entry,
289	    "version_bumped": check_version_bumped,
290	}
291	
292	
293	def run_checks(specs: tuple[CheckSpec, ...] | list[CheckSpec], entries: list[LedgerEntry],
294	               ws: Workspace) -> list[Check]:
295	    ctx = CheckContext(entries=entries, ws=ws)
296	    out: list[Check] = []
297	    for spec in specs:
298	        fn = CHECKS.get(spec.id)
299	        if fn is None:
300	            out.append(Check(id=spec.id, ok=False, weight=spec.weight, detail="unknown check id"))
301	            continue
302	        try:
303	            ok, detail = fn(ctx)
304	        # ordering matters: WorkspaceError IS an Exception, so it has to be caught first or the
305	        # generic handler below would turn "the sandbox is gone" into a failed check.
306	        except WorkspaceError:
307	            raise  # refuse to grade rather than score an unreadable workspace
308	        except Exception as exc:  # pragma: no cover - a broken check must not sink the grade
309	            ok, detail = False, f"check raised {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
310	        out.append(Check(id=spec.id, ok=ok, weight=spec.weight, detail=detail))
311	        log.info("grader.check", spec.id, check=spec.id, ok=ok, weight=spec.weight, detail=detail)
312	    return out
313	
314	
315	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- hidden tests
316	
317	
318	def parse_pytest(output: str) -> tuple[int, int, int]:
319	    """(passed, failed, errors) from a `pytest -q` summary line."""
320	    passed = failed = errors = 0
321	    for count, word in _SUMMARY_RE.findall(output or ""):
322	        n = int(count)
323	        if word == "passed":
324	            passed = n
325	        elif word == "failed":
326	            failed = n
327	        elif word.startswith("error"):
328	            errors = n
329	    return passed, failed, errors
330	
331	
332	def summary_found(output: str) -> bool:
333	    return bool(_SUMMARY_RE.search(output or ""))
334	
335	
336	def reconcile_exit_code(passed: int, failed: int, errors: int, exit_code: int) -> tuple[int, str]:
337	    """Cross-check the parsed summary against pytest's exit status.
338	
339	    Reading the summary alone is not enough: a collection error, a missing interpreter or a wrong
340	    test path can exit 0 (or 4/5) with no counts at all, which would otherwise be scored as "no
341	    failures" and hand the run 60 free points. Anything the summary cannot explain becomes an error.
342	    """
343	    if exit_code == 0 and passed > 0 and failed == 0 and errors == 0:
344	        return errors, ""
345	    if exit_code != 0 and (failed > 0 or errors > 0):
346	        return errors, ""
347	    if exit_code == 0 and passed == 0:
348	        return max(errors, 1), f"pytest exited 0 but collected nothing (exit_code={exit_code})"
349	    if exit_code != 0:
350	        return max(errors, 1), f"pytest exited {exit_code} with no failure in the summary"
351	    return errors, ""
352	
353	
354	def run_hidden_tests(ws: Workspace, bundle: ScenarioBundle) -> TestsResult:
355	    """Upload the hidden tests, run visible+hidden together, then remove them again."""
356	    hidden_dir = bundle.hidden_tests_dir
357	    uploaded = False
358	    if hidden_dir and hidden_dir.is_dir():
359	        ws.upload_tar(tar_bytes(str(hidden_dir)), abs_path(EVAL_DIR))
360	        uploaded = True
361	        log.info("grader.hidden_uploaded", str(hidden_dir), scenario_id=bundle.id, dest=abs_path(EVAL_DIR))
362	    else:
363	        log.warn("grader.hidden_missing", f"no hidden tests for {bundle.id}", scenario_id=bundle.id)
364	
365	    cmd = PYTEST_CMD if uploaded else f"cd {WORKSPACE} && PYTHONPATH={WORKSPACE}/src {_PYTEST} tests"
366	    t0 = time.perf_counter()
367	    try:
368	        res = ws.run(cmd, timeout_s=PYTEST_TIMEOUT_S)
369	        raw = (res.stdout or "") + (("\n" + res.stderr) if res.stderr else "")
370	        exit_code = res.exit_code
371	    except WorkspaceError:
372	        # NOT "0 tests passed". A sandbox we cannot reach is an *ungradeable* episode, and turning
373	        # that into a score would blame the agent for infrastructure — exactly the mislabelling
374	        # (run r_ccda8780cbee: status ok, score null, EINTERNAL) this whole taxonomy exists to end.
375	        log.error("grader.tests_unreachable", "sandbox unavailable while running the hidden tests")
376	        raise
377	    finally:
378	        if uploaded:
379	            try:
380	                ws.rmtree_abs(abs_path(EVAL_DIR))
381	                log.info("grader.hidden_removed", abs_path(EVAL_DIR))
382	            except Exception as exc:  # pragma: no cover
383	                log.warn("grader.hidden_remove_failed", str(exc))
384	
385	    passed, failed, errors = parse_pytest(raw)
386	    if not summary_found(raw):
387	        errors = max(errors, 1)
388	        raw += f"\n[faultline] pytest produced no summary line (exit_code={exit_code}); tests did not run"
389	    else:
390	        errors, note = reconcile_exit_code(passed, failed, errors, exit_code)
391	        if note:
392	            raw += f"\n[faultline] {note}"
393	    raw += f"\n[faultline] exit_code={exit_code} cmd={cmd}"
394	    output, _ = truncate_stdio(raw)
395	    log.info("grader.tests", "pytest finished", passed=passed, failed=failed, errors=errors,
396	             exit_code=exit_code, dur_ms=int((time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000))
397	    return TestsResult(passed=passed, failed=failed, errors=errors, output=output)
398	
399	
400	def tests_pass(tests: TestsResult) -> bool:
401	    return tests.failed == 0 and tests.errors == 0 and tests.passed > 0
402	
403	
404	def score_for(tests: TestsResult, checks: list[Check]) -> float:
405	    total_w = sum(c.weight for c in checks)
406	    recovery = (sum(c.weight for c in checks if c.ok) / total_w) if total_w else 1.0
407	    return round(60.0 * (1.0 if tests_pass(tests) else 0.0) + 40.0 * recovery, 1)
408	
409	
410	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- entry point
411	
412	
413	def evaluate(episode_id: str, ws: Workspace | None = None) -> EvaluateResponse:
414	    ep = episodes.load(episode_id)
415	    bundle = scenarios.get_bundle(ep["scenario_id"])
416	    t0 = time.perf_counter()
417	
418	    # A sandbox that cannot be reached cannot be graded *and* cannot be cleaned up by its owner
419	    # later, so record it as gone here (idempotent, and `delete` stays the only terminal
420	    # transition for a healthy episode). Without this the episode keeps claiming a live sandbox
421	    # in `GET /episodes` and only `reap` would ever reclaim it.
422	    try:
423	        ws = ws or episodes.workspace_for(ep)
424	        tests = run_hidden_tests(ws, bundle)
425	        entries = episodes.ledger_entries(ep)
426	        checks = run_checks(bundle.checks, entries, ws)
427	    except WorkspaceError as exc:
428	        log.error("episode.evaluate_failed", f"sandbox unavailable: {exc}",
429	                  episode_id=episode_id, scenario_id=bundle.id,
430	                  sandbox_id=ep.get("sandbox_id"))
431	        episodes.delete(episode_id)  # terminate (best effort) + mark terminated in the Dict
432	        raise
433	    score = score_for(tests, checks)
434	
435	    ep["evaluated_at"] = episodes.now_iso() if hasattr(episodes, "now_iso") else None
436	    ep["score"] = score
437	    episodes.save(ep)
438	
439	    log.info(
440	        "episode.evaluated",
441	        f"score {score}",
442	        episode_id=episode_id,
443	        scenario_id=bundle.id,
444	        score=score,
445	        passed=tests_pass(tests),
446	        tests=f"{tests.passed}p/{tests.failed}f/{tests.errors}e",
447	        checks_ok=sum(1 for c in checks if c.ok),
448	        checks_total=len(checks),
449	        steps=ep.get("step", 0),
450	        dur_ms=int((time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000),
451	    )
452	    return EvaluateResponse(
453	        episode_id=episode_id,
454	        score=score,
455	        passed=tests_pass(tests),
456	        checks=checks,
457	        tests=tests,
458	        ledger=entries,
459	    )
460	
461	
462	__all__ = [
463	    "evaluate", "run_checks", "run_hidden_tests", "parse_pytest", "score_for", "tests_pass",
464	    "CHECKS", "CheckContext", "entry_touches", "entry_is_read", "entry_is_failing", "find_fault",
465	    "find_interrupted",
466	]

Tool reply2026-09-13T00:37:46.274Z
1	"""Scenario catalogue.
2	
3	A scenario file has a *public* half and a *private* half, and `ScenarioBundle` keeps the two apart
4	so it is hard to leak the private half by accident: the REST layer only ever serialises
5	`bundle.public`.
6	
7	Public (`Scenario`, safe for the browser; only `task_prompt` ever reaches the model):
8	    id · title · description · task_prompt · max_steps · fault_kinds (kinds only) ·
9	    harness_faults (REAL interruptions the harness inflicts on itself) ·
10	    checks (id/description/weight — what the grader will look for) ·
11	    faults_public (one row per fault class: kind + origin + layer + a sentence)
12	
13	Private (never leaves this service): the fault plan's paths, modes, hit counts and delays, the
14	setup overlays, the check weights' arithmetic, and the hidden grader tests.
15	
16	Data lives in three directories that are baked into the Modal image under /app; locally they sit
17	in the service tree. Both layouts are probed, so the same code runs in a container and in pytest.
18	"""
19	
20	from __future__ import annotations
21	
22	import functools
23	import json
24	from dataclasses import dataclass
25	from pathlib import Path
26	from typing import Any
27	
28	from faultline_common.schemas import CheckSpec as PublicCheckSpec
29	from faultline_common.schemas import FaultKind, FaultPlan, HarnessFault, Scenario
30	
31	from . import provenance
32	
33	_HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
34	_SERVICE_ROOT = _HERE.parent
35	
36	
37	def _first_existing(*candidates: Path) -> Path:
38	    for c in candidates:
39	        if c.is_dir():
40	            return c
41	    return candidates[-1]
42	
43	
44	#: scenario JSON + overlays/ (image: /app/scenarios, local: sandbox_env/scenarios)
45	SCENARIOS_DIR = _first_existing(Path("/app/scenarios"), _HERE / "scenarios")
46	#: fixture repos copied into the sandbox at reset (image: /app/fixtures)
47	FIXTURES_DIR = _first_existing(Path("/app/fixtures"), _SERVICE_ROOT / "fixtures")
48	#: hidden grader tests, uploaded only at evaluate time (image: /app/graders)
49	GRADERS_DIR = _first_existing(Path("/app/graders"), _SERVICE_ROOT / "graders")
50	
51	
52	class ScenarioNotFound(KeyError):
53	    pass
54	
55	
56	@dataclass(frozen=True)
57	class SetupOp:
58	    """A pre-episode edit applied to the fixture before the agent ever sees it."""
59	
60	    op: str
61	    path: str
62	    content: bytes
63	
64	
65	@dataclass(frozen=True)
66	class CheckSpec:
67	    id: str
68	    weight: float
69	    description: str = ""
70	
71	
72	@dataclass(frozen=True)
73	class ScenarioBundle:
74	    public: Scenario
75	    fixture: str
76	    setup: tuple[SetupOp, ...]
77	    fault_plan: FaultPlan
78	    checks: tuple[CheckSpec, ...]
79	    hidden_tests: str | None
80	
81	    @property
82	    def id(self) -> str:
83	        return self.public.id
84	
85	    @property
86	    def fixture_dir(self) -> Path:
87	        return FIXTURES_DIR / self.fixture
88	
89	    @property
90	    def hidden_tests_dir(self) -> Path | None:
91	        if not self.hidden_tests:
92	            return None
93	        rel = self.hidden_tests
94	        # scenario files say "graders/<id>"; the image drops the "graders/" prefix
95	        if rel.startswith("graders/"):
96	            return GRADERS_DIR / rel[len("graders/") :]
97	        p = Path(rel)
98	        return p if p.is_absolute() else _SERVICE_ROOT / p
99	
100	
101	def _load_one(path: Path) -> ScenarioBundle:
102	    raw: dict[str, Any] = json.loads(path.read_text())
103	    plan = FaultPlan.model_validate(raw.get("fault_plan") or {})
104	    kinds: list[FaultKind] = []
105	    for f in plan.faults:
106	        if f.kind not in kinds:
107	            kinds.append(f.kind)
108	    # REAL interruptions the harness inflicts on itself. They are public whole (path and timing
109	    # included) because they are a chaos setting for the *run*, executed by the harness, not a trap
110	    # laid for the agent — nothing here reaches the model, which only ever sees `task_prompt`.
111	    harness_faults = [HarnessFault.model_validate(h) for h in raw.get("harness_faults") or []]
112	    public = Scenario(
113	        id=raw["id"],
114	        title=raw["title"],
115	        description=raw.get("description", ""),
116	        task_prompt=raw["task_prompt"],
117	        max_steps=int(raw.get("max_steps", 20)),
118	        fault_kinds=kinds,
119	        harness_faults=harness_faults,
120	        # what the grader will look for: id/description/weight, no thresholds, no ledger internals
121	        checks=[
122	            PublicCheckSpec(
123	                id=c["id"],
124	                description=c.get("description", ""),
125	                weight=float(c.get("weight", 1.0)),
126	            )
127	            for c in raw.get("checks") or []
128	        ],
129	        # the fault *classes* in play, with provenance — never paths, hits, modes or timings
130	        faults_public=provenance.faults_public(plan, harness_faults),
131	    )
132	    setup: list[SetupOp] = []
133	    for op in raw.get("setup") or []:
134	        if op.get("op") != "write":
135	            raise ValueError(f"{path.name}: unsupported setup op {op.get('op')!r}")
136	        if "content_file" in op:
137	            src = (SCENARIOS_DIR / op["content_file"]).resolve()
138	            content = src.read_bytes()
139	        else:
140	            content = str(op.get("content", "")).encode()
141	        setup.append(SetupOp(op="write", path=op["path"], content=content))
142	    checks = tuple(
143	        CheckSpec(id=c["id"], weight=float(c.get("weight", 1.0)), description=c.get("description", ""))
144	        for c in raw.get("checks") or []
145	    )
146	    return ScenarioBundle(
147	        public=public,
148	        fixture=raw.get("fixture", "ratelimiter"),
149	        setup=tuple(setup),
150	        fault_plan=plan,
151	        checks=checks,
152	        hidden_tests=raw.get("hidden_tests"),
153	    )
154	
155	
156	@functools.lru_cache(maxsize=1)
157	def _catalogue() -> dict[str, ScenarioBundle]:
158	    out: dict[str, ScenarioBundle] = {}
159	    for p in sorted(SCENARIOS_DIR.glob("*.json")):
160	        b = _load_one(p)
161	        out[b.id] = b
162	    return out
163	
164	
165	def reload_catalogue() -> None:
166	    """Drop the cache (tests only)."""
167	    _catalogue.cache_clear()
168	
169	
170	def list_bundles() -> list[ScenarioBundle]:
171	    return list(_catalogue().values())
172	
173	
174	def list_scenarios() -> list[Scenario]:
175	    """Public catalogue: what the UI and the agent are allowed to see."""
176	    return [b.public for b in _catalogue().values()]
177	
178	
179	def get_bundle(scenario_id: str) -> ScenarioBundle:
180	    try:
181	        return _catalogue()[scenario_id]
182	    except KeyError as exc:
183	        raise ScenarioNotFound(scenario_id) from exc

Tool reply2026-09-13T00:37:54.651Z
1	"""The exec seam: everything that talks to a real Modal Sandbox lives here, behind one protocol.
2	
3	Two implementations:
4	  * :class:`ModalWorkspace` — a live `modal.Sandbox`. Every filesystem operation goes through a
5	    single helper script uploaded to /opt at reset, so one logical operation costs exactly one
6	    `sb.exec` round trip and always answers with JSON (no fragile stdout parsing).
7	  * :class:`FakeWorkspace` — an in-memory tree used by the unit tests, so the fault engine, the
8	    ledger, the grader and the MCP surface are all testable with no Modal and no network.
9	
10	Absolute paths inside the sandbox:
11	    /workspace                 the agent's repo (the ONLY thing it is told about)
12	    /opt/faultline_baseline    pristine copy taken at reset; `observe` diffs against it
13	    /opt/faultline_helper.py   this module's helper script
14	"""
15	
16	from __future__ import annotations
17	
18	import base64
19	import difflib
20	import hashlib
21	import io
22	import json
23	import os
24	import posixpath
25	import tarfile
26	import time
27	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
28	from typing import Any, Literal, Protocol
29	
30	from faultline_common.log import get_logger
31	
32	from .paths import BASELINE, HELPER, WORKSPACE, abs_path
33	
34	log = get_logger("sandbox-env")
35	
36	#: names never shown to the agent and never hashed into the baseline
37	SKIP_DIRS = ("__pycache__", ".git", ".pytest_cache", ".faultline_eval")
38	SKIP_SUFFIXES = (".pyc",)
39	
40	
41	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- result types
42	
43	@dataclass
44	class ExecResult:
45	    stdout: str = ""
46	    stderr: str = ""
47	    exit_code: int = 0
48	    duration_ms: int = 0
49	    timed_out: bool = False
50	
51	
52	@dataclass
53	class ReadResult:
54	    kind: Literal["file", "dir", "missing", "denied"] = "file"
55	    content: str = ""
56	    size: int = 0
57	    sha256: str = ""
58	    truncated: bool = False
59	
60	
61	@dataclass
62	class WriteResult:
63	    kind: Literal["ok", "denied", "isdir"] = "ok"
64	    bytes_written: int = 0
65	    size: int = 0
66	    sha256: str = ""
67	
68	
69	@dataclass
70	class Entry:
71	    name: str
72	    type: Literal["file", "dir", "other"]
73	    size: int | None = None
74	
75	
76	@dataclass
77	class ListResult:
78	    kind: Literal["ok", "missing", "notdir"] = "ok"
79	    entries: list[Entry] = field(default_factory=list)
80	
81	
82	class WorkspaceError(RuntimeError):
83	    """The sandbox itself failed (terminated, unreachable, helper crashed)."""
84	
85	
86	class Workspace(Protocol):
87	    """Everything sandbox-env needs from a command sandbox."""
88	
89	    sandbox_id: str
90	
91	    def run(self, command: str, timeout_s: int) -> ExecResult: ...
92	    def read(self, rel: str, cap: int) -> ReadResult: ...
93	    def write(self, rel: str, content: str, append: bool) -> WriteResult: ...
94	    def listdir(self, rel: str) -> ListResult: ...
95	    def sha_map(self, root: str = WORKSPACE) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]: ...
96	    def diffs(self, rels: list[str], cap: int) -> list[dict[str, str]]: ...
97	    def upload_tar(self, data: bytes, dest: str) -> None: ...
98	    def snapshot_baseline(self) -> None: ...
99	    def remove(self, rel: str) -> None: ...
100	    def rmtree_abs(self, path: str) -> None: ...
101	    def terminate(self) -> None: ...
102	    def alive(self) -> bool: ...
103	
104	
105	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- helper script
106	
107	#: Runs *inside* the sandbox (python3.11, stdlib only). One subcommand per logical operation;
108	#: always prints a single JSON object/array on stdout. Kept as source text so the image needs
109	#: nothing but python.
110	HELPER_SOURCE = r'''
111	import base64, difflib, hashlib, json, os, shutil, sys, tarfile
112	
113	SKIP_DIRS = {"__pycache__", ".git", ".pytest_cache", ".faultline_eval"}
114	
115	def out(obj):
116	    sys.stdout.write(json.dumps(obj))
117	    sys.stdout.flush()
118	
119	def walk(root):
120	    res = {}
121	    for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(root):
122	        dirnames[:] = [d for d in dirnames if d not in SKIP_DIRS]
123	        for fn in filenames:
124	            if fn.endswith(".pyc"):
125	                continue
126	            fp = os.path.join(dirpath, fn)
127	            rel = os.path.relpath(fp, root)
128	            try:
129	                with open(fp, "rb") as f:
130	                    data = f.read()
131	            except OSError:
132	                continue
133	            res[rel] = {"sha256": hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest(), "size": len(data)}
134	    return res
135	
136	def read_text(p):
137	    try:
138	        with open(p, "r", errors="replace") as f:
139	            return f.read().splitlines(keepends=True)
140	    except OSError:
141	        return []
142	
143	def main(argv):
144	    op = argv[0]
145	    if op == "read":
146	        p, cap = argv[1], int(argv[2])
147	        if os.path.isdir(p):
148	            return out({"kind": "dir"})
149	        try:
150	            with open(p, "rb") as f:
151	                data = f.read()
152	        except FileNotFoundError:
153	            return out({"kind": "missing"})
154	        except IsADirectoryError:
155	            return out({"kind": "dir"})
156	        except PermissionError:
157	            return out({"kind": "denied"})
158	        text = data.decode("utf-8", "replace")
159	        return out({"kind": "file", "size": len(data),
160	                    "sha256": hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest(),
161	                    "truncated": len(text) > cap, "content": text[:cap]})
162	    if op == "write":
163	        p, mode, src = argv[1], argv[2], argv[3]
164	        data = open(src[1:], "rb").read() if src.startswith("@") else base64.b64decode(src)
165	        if src.startswith("@"):
166	            try:
167	                os.remove(src[1:])
168	            except OSError:
169	                pass
170	        d = os.path.dirname(p)
171	        if d:
172	            os.makedirs(d, exist_ok=True)
173	        try:
174	            with open(p, "ab" if mode == "append" else "wb") as f:
175	                f.write(data)
176	        except PermissionError:
177	            return out({"kind": "denied"})
178	        except IsADirectoryError:
179	            return out({"kind": "isdir"})
180	        with open(p, "rb") as f:
181	            whole = f.read()
182	        return out({"kind": "ok", "bytes_written": len(data), "size": len(whole),
183	                    "sha256": hashlib.sha256(whole).hexdigest()})
184	    if op == "list":
185	        p = argv[1]
186	        if not os.path.exists(p):
187	            return out({"kind": "missing"})
188	        if not os.path.isdir(p):
189	            return out({"kind": "notdir"})
190	        entries = []
191	        for name in sorted(os.listdir(p)):
192	            if name in SKIP_DIRS:
193	                continue
194	            fp = os.path.join(p, name)
195	            if os.path.isdir(fp):
196	                entries.append({"name": name, "type": "dir", "size": None})
197	            elif os.path.isfile(fp):
198	                entries.append({"name": name, "type": "file", "size": os.path.getsize(fp)})
199	            else:
200	                entries.append({"name": name, "type": "other", "size": None})
201	        return out({"kind": "ok", "entries": entries})
202	    if op == "shamap":
203	        return out(walk(argv[1]))
204	    if op == "diff":
205	        base, work, cap = argv[1], argv[2], int(argv[4])
206	        rels = json.loads(argv[3])
207	        res = []
208	        for rel in rels:
209	            a = read_text(os.path.join(base, rel))
210	            b = read_text(os.path.join(work, rel))
211	            u = "".join(difflib.unified_diff(a, b, fromfile="a/" + rel, tofile="b/" + rel))
212	            if len(u) > cap:
213	                u = u[:cap] + "\n...[diff truncated]\n"
214	            res.append({"path": rel, "unified": u})
215	        return out(res)
216	    if op == "untar":
217	        tar, dest = argv[1], argv[2]
218	        os.makedirs(dest, exist_ok=True)
219	        with tarfile.open(tar) as tf:
220	            try:
221	                tf.extractall(dest, filter="data")
222	            except TypeError:
223	                tf.extractall(dest)
224	        os.remove(tar)
225	        return out({"kind": "ok"})
226	    if op == "snapshot":
227	        src, dst = argv[1], argv[2]
228	        if os.path.exists(dst):
229	            shutil.rmtree(dst)
230	        shutil.copytree(src, dst, ignore=shutil.ignore_patterns(*SKIP_DIRS, "*.pyc"))
231	        return out({"kind": "ok"})
232	    if op == "rm":
233	        try:
234	            os.remove(argv[1])
235	        except FileNotFoundError:
236	            pass
237	        return out({"kind": "ok"})
238	    if op == "rmtree":
239	        shutil.rmtree(argv[1], ignore_errors=True)
240	        return out({"kind": "ok"})
241	    if op == "cptree":
242	        src, dst = argv[1], argv[2]
243	        if os.path.exists(dst):
244	            shutil.rmtree(dst)
245	        shutil.copytree(src, dst, ignore=shutil.ignore_patterns(*SKIP_DIRS, "*.pyc"))
246	        return out({"kind": "ok"})
247	    return out({"kind": "error", "detail": "unknown op " + op})
248	
249	try:
250	    main(sys.argv[1:])
251	except Exception as exc:  # never let the helper die silently
252	    out({"kind": "error", "detail": "%s: %s" % (type(exc).__name__, exc)})
253	'''
254	
255	
256	def tar_bytes(local_dir: str, arcname: str = ".") -> bytes:
257	    """Pack a local directory (fixture, hidden tests) into an in-memory tar, minus build junk."""
258	    buf = io.BytesIO()
259	
260	    def _filter(ti: tarfile.TarInfo) -> tarfile.TarInfo | None:
261	        parts = ti.name.split("/")
262	        if any(p in SKIP_DIRS for p in parts):
263	            return None
264	        if ti.name.endswith(SKIP_SUFFIXES):
265	            return None
266	        ti.uid = ti.gid = 0
267	        ti.uname = ti.gname = "root"
268	        return ti
269	
270	    with tarfile.open(fileobj=buf, mode="w") as tf:
271	        tf.add(local_dir, arcname=arcname, filter=_filter)
272	    return buf.getvalue()
273	
274	
275	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- modal implementation
276	
277	
278	class ModalWorkspace:
279	    """A live `modal.Sandbox`. All filesystem ops go through the uploaded helper script."""
280	
281	    def __init__(self, sandbox: Any):
282	        self._sb = sandbox
283	        self.sandbox_id = getattr(sandbox, "object_id", "") or ""
284	
285	    # -- plumbing ---------------------------------------------------------
286	
287	    def _exec(self, argv: list[str], timeout_s: int | None = 60, workdir: str | None = None,
288	              env: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> ExecResult:
289	        t0 = time.perf_counter()
290	        try:
291	            proc = self._sb.exec(*argv, timeout=timeout_s, workdir=workdir, env=env, text=True)
292	            stdout = proc.stdout.read()
293	            stderr = proc.stderr.read()
294	            rc = proc.wait()
295	        except Exception as exc:  # sandbox gone, exec timeout, grpc hiccup
296	            dur = int((time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000)
297	            name = type(exc).__name__
298	            timed_out = "Timeout" in name or "timeout" in str(exc).lower()
299	            log.warn("sandbox.exec_failed", str(exc), sandbox_id=self.sandbox_id,
300	                     argv0=argv[0], err=name, dur_ms=dur)
301	            if timed_out:
302	                return ExecResult(stderr=f"{name}: {exc}", exit_code=124, duration_ms=dur, timed_out=True)
303	            raise WorkspaceError(f"{name}: {exc}") from exc
304	        dur = int((time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000)
305	        return ExecResult(stdout=stdout or "", stderr=stderr or "", exit_code=int(rc or 0), duration_ms=dur)
306	
307	    def _helper(self, *args: str, timeout_s: int = 120) -> Any:
308	        res = self._exec(["python3", HELPER, *args], timeout_s=timeout_s)
309	        raw = (res.stdout or "").strip()
310	        if not raw:
311	            raise WorkspaceError(f"helper {args[0]} produced no output (rc={res.exit_code}) {res.stderr[:400]}")
312	        try:
313	            parsed = json.loads(raw)
314	        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
315	            raise WorkspaceError(f"helper {args[0]} returned non-JSON: {raw[:400]}") from exc
316	        if isinstance(parsed, dict) and parsed.get("kind") == "error":
317	            raise WorkspaceError(f"helper {args[0]} failed: {parsed.get('detail')}")
318	        return parsed
319	
320	    def put_bytes(self, remote_path: str, data: bytes) -> None:
321	        # `sb.open()` / FileIO is deprecated and unsupported by the Sandbox v2 backend (the default
322	        # from modal 1.6), so the filesystem namespace is the only forward-compatible upload path.
323	        try:
324	            self._sb.filesystem.write_bytes(data, remote_path)
325	        except AttributeError:  # pragma: no cover - ancient client
326	            with self._sb.open(remote_path, "wb") as f:
327	                f.write(data)
328	        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
329	            raise WorkspaceError(f"upload to {remote_path} failed: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}") from exc
330	
331	    def install_helper(self) -> None:
332	        self._exec(["mkdir", "-p", posixpath.dirname(HELPER)], timeout_s=30)
333	        self.put_bytes(HELPER, HELPER_SOURCE.encode())
334	
335	    # -- Workspace protocol ----------------------------------------------
336	
337	    def run(self, command: str, timeout_s: int) -> ExecResult:
338	        return self._exec(
339	            ["bash", "-lc", command],
340	            timeout_s=timeout_s,
341	            workdir=WORKSPACE,
342	            env={"PYTHONPATH": f"{WORKSPACE}/src", "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1"},
343	        )
344	
345	    def read(self, rel: str, cap: int) -> ReadResult:
346	        d = self._helper("read", abs_path(rel), str(cap))
347	        return ReadResult(
348	            kind=d.get("kind", "missing"),
349	            content=d.get("content", ""),
350	            size=int(d.get("size", 0)),
351	            sha256=d.get("sha256", ""),
352	            truncated=bool(d.get("truncated", False)),
353	        )
354	
355	    def write(self, rel: str, content: str, append: bool) -> WriteResult:
356	        data = content.encode()
357	        b64 = base64.b64encode(data).decode()
358	        mode = "append" if append else "overwrite"
359	        if len(b64) > 90_000:  # Linux caps a single argv string at 128 KiB
360	            staging = f"/tmp/.faultline_write_{hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()[:12]}"
361	            self.put_bytes(staging, data)
362	            src = "@" + staging
363	        else:
364	            src = b64
365	        d = self._helper("write", abs_path(rel), mode, src)
366	        return WriteResult(
367	            kind=d.get("kind", "ok"),
368	            bytes_written=int(d.get("bytes_written", 0)),
369	            size=int(d.get("size", 0)),
370	            sha256=d.get("sha256", ""),
371	        )
372	
373	    def listdir(self, rel: str) -> ListResult:
374	        d = self._helper("list", abs_path(rel))
375	        if d.get("kind") != "ok":
376	            return ListResult(kind=d.get("kind", "missing"))
377	        return ListResult(kind="ok", entries=[Entry(**e) for e in d.get("entries", [])])
378	
379	    def sha_map(self, root: str = WORKSPACE) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:
380	        return self._helper("shamap", root)
381	
382	    def diffs(self, rels: list[str], cap: int) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
383	        if not rels:
384	            return []
385	        return self._helper("diff", BASELINE, WORKSPACE, json.dumps(rels), str(cap))
386	
387	    def upload_tar(self, data: bytes, dest: str) -> None:
388	        remote = f"/tmp/.faultline_{hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()[:12]}.tar"
389	        self.put_bytes(remote, data)
390	        self._helper("untar", remote, dest)
391	
392	    def snapshot_baseline(self) -> None:
393	        self._helper("snapshot", WORKSPACE, BASELINE)
394	
395	    def remove(self, rel: str) -> None:
396	        self._helper("rm", abs_path(rel))
397	
398	    def rmtree_abs(self, path: str) -> None:
399	        self._helper("rmtree", path)
400	
401	    def terminate(self) -> None:
402	        try:
403	            self._sb.terminate(wait=False)
404	        except TypeError:
405	            self._sb.terminate()
406	
407	    def alive(self) -> bool:
408	        """Is the sandbox still running? `poll()` returns None while it is, else an exit code.
409	
410	        Used only by the ops listing and the TTL sweep, so a probe that itself fails (sandbox
411	        already garbage-collected, transient grpc error) answers "not alive" instead of raising:
412	        the caller's next move for either answer is the same, and an ops route must never 500.
413	        """
414	        try:
415	            return self._sb.poll() is None
416	        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
417	            log.debug("sandbox.poll_failed", str(exc), sandbox_id=self.sandbox_id)
418	            return False
419	
420	
421	# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- fake (tests)
422	
423	
424	class FakeWorkspace:
425	    """In-memory workspace used by the unit tests and by `scripts/` dry runs.
426	
427	    `run()` is not a shell: it understands just enough (`cat`, `ls`, `echo >`, `pytest`) to drive
428	    the ledger, and every command can be pre-scripted via `responses`.
429	    """
430	
431	    def __init__(self, files: dict[str, str] | None = None, sandbox_id: str = "sb-fake"):
432	        self.files: dict[str, str] = dict(files or {})
433	        self.baseline: dict[str, str] = {}
434	        self.sandbox_id = sandbox_id
435	        self.terminated = False
436	        self.commands: list[str] = []
437	        self.responses: dict[str, ExecResult] = {}
438	        self.default_exec = ExecResult(stdout="", stderr="", exit_code=0, duration_ms=1)
439	
440	    # -- helpers ---------------------------------------------------------
441	
442	    @staticmethod
443	    def _sha(text: str) -> str:
444	        return hashlib.sha256(text.encode()).hexdigest()
445	
446	    # -- Workspace protocol ----------------------------------------------
447	
448	    def run(self, command: str, timeout_s: int) -> ExecResult:
449	        self.commands.append(command)
450	        if command in self.responses:
451	            return self.responses[command]
452	        for key, res in self.responses.items():
453	            if key in command:
454	                return res
455	        return ExecResult(**{**self.default_exec.__dict__})
456	
457	    def read(self, rel: str, cap: int) -> ReadResult:
458	        if rel in self.files:
459	            text = self.files[rel]
460	            return ReadResult(kind="file", content=text[:cap], size=len(text.encode()),
461	                              sha256=self._sha(text), truncated=len(text) > cap)
462	        if any(f.startswith(rel.rstrip("/") + "/") for f in self.files):
463	            return ReadResult(kind="dir")
464	        return ReadResult(kind="missing")
465	
466	    def write(self, rel: str, content: str, append: bool) -> WriteResult:
467	        whole = (self.files.get(rel, "") + content) if append else content
468	        self.files[rel] = whole
469	        return WriteResult(kind="ok", bytes_written=len(content.encode()),
470	                           size=len(whole.encode()), sha256=self._sha(whole))
471	
472	    def listdir(self, rel: str) -> ListResult:
473	        prefix = "" if rel in ("", ".") else rel.rstrip("/") + "/"
474	        if prefix and not any(f.startswith(prefix) for f in self.files):
475	            return ListResult(kind="missing")
476	        names: dict[str, Entry] = {}
477	        for f in sorted(self.files):
478	            if not f.startswith(prefix):
479	                continue
480	            rest = f[len(prefix) :]
481	            head, _, tail = rest.partition("/")
482	            if tail:
483	                names.setdefault(head, Entry(name=head, type="dir"))
484	            else:
485	                names[head] = Entry(name=head, type="file", size=len(self.files[f].encode()))
486	        return ListResult(kind="ok", entries=[names[k] for k in sorted(names)])
487	
488	    def sha_map(self, root: str = WORKSPACE) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:
489	        src = self.baseline if root == BASELINE else self.files
490	        return {p: {"sha256": self._sha(t), "size": len(t.encode())} for p, t in src.items()}
491	
492	    def diffs(self, rels: list[str], cap: int) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
493	        out = []
494	        for rel in rels:
495	            a = self.baseline.get(rel, "").splitlines(keepends=True)
496	            b = self.files.get(rel, "").splitlines(keepends=True)
497	            u = "".join(difflib.unified_diff(a, b, fromfile="a/" + rel, tofile="b/" + rel))
498	            out.append({"path": rel, "unified": u[:cap]})
499	        return out
500	
501	    def upload_tar(self, data: bytes, dest: str) -> None:
502	        with tarfile.open(fileobj=io.BytesIO(data)) as tf:
503	            for member in tf.getmembers():
504	                if not member.isfile():
505	                    continue
506	                fh = tf.extractfile(member)
507	                if fh is None:
508	                    continue
509	                rel = posixpath.normpath(member.name)
510	                if dest != WORKSPACE:
511	                    rel = posixpath.join(posixpath.relpath(dest, WORKSPACE), rel)
512	                self.files[posixpath.normpath(rel)] = fh.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
513	
514	    def snapshot_baseline(self) -> None:
515	        self.baseline = dict(self.files)
516	
517	    def remove(self, rel: str) -> None:
518	        self.files.pop(rel, None)
519	
520	    def rmtree_abs(self, path: str) -> None:
521	        rel = path[len(WORKSPACE) + 1 :] if path.startswith(WORKSPACE + "/") else path
522	        for f in list(self.files):
523	            if f == rel or f.startswith(rel.rstrip("/") + "/"):
524	                del self.files[f]
525	
526	    def terminate(self) -> None:
527	        self.terminated = True
528	
529	    def alive(self) -> bool:
530	        return not self.terminated
531	
532	
533	def local_tar_of(path: str) -> bytes:
534	    """Thin wrapper so callers do not import tarfile."""
535	    return tar_bytes(path)
536

540	#: the *command* sandbox image. Deliberately boring — a plain dev box with python + pytest — so
541	#: nothing inside it hints that failures are being injected one layer up.
542	SANDBOX_APP_NAME = os.environ.get("FAULTLINE_SANDBOX_APP", "faultline-sandboxes")
543	SANDBOX_TIMEOUT_S = int(os.environ.get("FAULTLINE_SANDBOX_TIMEOUT", "1800"))
544	SANDBOX_IDLE_TIMEOUT_S = int(os.environ.get("FAULTLINE_SANDBOX_IDLE_TIMEOUT", "600"))

547	def sandbox_image():
548	    """Built at deploy time by the `prewarm` function in modal_app.py so reset stays fast."""
549	    import modal
550	
551	    return (
552	        modal.Image.debian_slim(python_version="3.11")
553	        .pip_install("pytest==7.4.4")
554	        .run_commands(
555	            "mkdir -p /workspace /opt",
556	            'ln -sf "$(command -v python3)" /usr/local/bin/python || true',
557	        )
558	        .env({"PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1"})

562	def create_workspace() -> "Workspace":
563	    """Provision a fresh command sandbox and install the helper. Monkeypatched in tests."""
564	    import modal
565	
566	    t0 = time.perf_counter()
567	    sandbox_app = modal.App.lookup(SANDBOX_APP_NAME, create_if_missing=True)
568	    sb = modal.Sandbox.create(
569	        app=sandbox_app,
570	        image=sandbox_image(),
571	        workdir=WORKSPACE,
572	        timeout=SANDBOX_TIMEOUT_S,
573	        idle_timeout=SANDBOX_IDLE_TIMEOUT_S,
574	        cpu=1,
575	        memory=1024,
576	        block_network=True,  # the agent's shell has no egress at all
577	    )
578	    ws = ModalWorkspace(sb)
579	    log.info(
580	        "sandbox.created", "command sandbox up",
581	        sandbox_id=ws.sandbox_id, dur_ms=int((time.perf_counter() - t0) * 1000),
582	        block_network=True, timeout_s=SANDBOX_TIMEOUT_S, idle_timeout_s=SANDBOX_IDLE_TIMEOUT_S,
583	    )
584	    ws.install_helper()
585	    return ws

590	    import modal

592	    try:
593	        return ModalWorkspace(modal.Sandbox.from_id(sandbox_id))
594	    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
595	        raise WorkspaceError(f"sandbox {sandbox_id} is unreachable: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}") from exc

"""Faultline sandbox-env: the gym (reset/observe/evaluate) + the MCP tool surface (step).

Layout
------
paths.py      workspace-relative path safety (EINVAL on escapes)
faults.py     PURE fault engine: touches()/mutating()/decide(); no I/O, deterministic

=== sandbox_env/util.py
"""Small shared helpers: ids, timestamps, digests, and the truncation policy."""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
import uuid
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Any

from faultline_common.schemas import CONTENT_CAP, STDIO_CAP

#: keep the start (where the command echoes what it did) and the end (where the failure is)
STDIO_HEAD, STDIO_TAIL = 5_000, 3_000
CONTENT_HEAD, CONTENT_TAIL = 24_000, 8_000
_HINT = "use head/tail/grep to page through the rest"


def now_iso() -> str:
    return datetime.now(timezone.utc).isoformat(timespec="milliseconds").replace("+00:00", "Z")


def new_id(prefix: str) -> str:
    return f"{prefix}_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]}"


def sha256_hex(data: bytes) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()


def args_digest(args: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
    """sha256[:12] of the canonical JSON args — the ledger's 'same call again?' key."""
    canonical = json.dumps(args, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), default=str)
    return hashlib.sha256(canonical.encode()).hexdigest()[:12]


def truncate_middle(text: str, cap: int, head: int, tail: int) -> tuple[str, bool]:
    """Cap a blob by keeping its head and tail with an explicit elision marker.

    Chopping only the tail hides the traceback that explains the failure; chopping only the head
    hides the command echo. Keeping both ends (and saying how much went missing) is what makes a
    truncated tool result still actionable for the model.
    """
    if text is None:
        return "", False
    if len(text) <= cap:
        return text, False
    elided = len(text) - head - tail
    if elided <= 0:  # cap smaller than head+tail: degrade to a plain head cut
        return text[:cap] + f"\n...<elided {len(text) - cap} chars; {_HINT}>...\n", True
    return (
        text[:head] + f"\n...<elided {elided} chars; {_HINT}>...\n" + text[len(text) - tail :],
        True,
    )


def truncate_stdio(text: str) -> tuple[str, bool]:
    return truncate_middle(text, STDIO_CAP, STDIO_HEAD, STDIO_TAIL)


def truncate_content(text: str) -> tuple[str, bool]:
    return truncate_middle(text, CONTENT_CAP, CONTENT_HEAD, CONTENT_TAIL)


def drop_lines_mentioning(text: str, names: list[str]) -> str:
    """Best-effort removal of a vanished filename from a directory listing.

    A transient `missing_file` fault must look like the file is not there: if `read_file README.md`
    says ENOENT, `ls` must not list it in the same breath. We only touch lines that name the file.
    """
    if not names:
        return text
    keep = []
    for line in text.splitlines(keepends=True):
        stripped = line.strip()
        if any(_line_names(stripped, n) for n in names):
            continue
        keep.append(line)
    return "".join(keep)


def _line_names(line: str, rel: str) -> bool:
    base = rel.rsplit("/", 1)[-1]
    if not base:
        return False
    tokens = line.replace("\t", " ").split(" ")
    for t in tokens:
        t = t.strip().strip("'\"")
        if not t:
            continue
        t = t[2:] if t.startswith("./") else t
        if t == base or t == rel or t.endswith("/" + base):
            return True
    return False
=== sandbox_env/paths.py
"""Workspace path safety.

Every path the agent hands us is workspace-relative. Absolute paths are accepted only when they
live under /workspace; anything else, and any `..` component, is EINVAL before we touch the
sandbox. Pure module, no I/O.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import posixpath

WORKSPACE = "/workspace"
#: baseline copy of the pristine fixture; deliberately OUTSIDE the workspace so `observe`
#: can diff against it and the agent never sees it in its tree.
BASELINE = "/opt/faultline_baseline"
#: helper script uploaded at reset; runs file ops/diffing inside the sandbox in one round trip.
HELPER = "/opt/faultline_helper.py"
#: hidden tests are copied here at evaluate time and removed immediately afterwards.
EVAL_DIR = ".faultline_eval"


class PathError(ValueError):
    """Raised for a path that escapes the workspace. Callers map this to ToolError(EINVAL)."""

    def __init__(self, path: str, reason: str):
        self.path = path
        self.reason = reason
        super().__init__(f"{path}: {reason}")


def normalize_rel(path: str | None) -> str:
    """Return a clean workspace-relative path ('.' for the root), or raise PathError.

    Accepts: 'README.md', './README.md', 'src/x.py', '/workspace/src/x.py', '/workspace', ''.
    Rejects: '/etc/passwd', '../escape', 'src/../../escape', anything with a NUL byte.
    """
    raw = "" if path is None else str(path)
    if "\x00" in raw:
        raise PathError(raw, "invalid path")
    p = raw.strip()
    if not p:
        return "."
    if p.startswith("/"):
        if p == WORKSPACE:
            p = "."
        elif p.startswith(WORKSPACE + "/"):
            p = p[len(WORKSPACE) + 1 :]
        else:
            raise PathError(raw, f"path outside {WORKSPACE} is not allowed")
    # reject any explicit parent traversal, even one that would normalise back inside
    parts = [seg for seg in p.split("/") if seg not in ("", ".")]
    if any(seg == ".." for seg in parts):
        raise PathError(raw, "'..' traversal is not allowed")
    norm = posixpath.normpath("/".join(parts)) if parts else "."
    if norm.startswith("/") or norm.startswith(".."):
        raise PathError(raw, "path escapes the workspace")
    return norm or "."


def abs_path(rel: str) -> str:
    """Workspace-relative -> absolute path inside the sandbox."""
    rel = rel.strip()
    if rel in ("", "."):
        return WORKSPACE
    if rel.startswith("/"):
        return rel
    return posixpath.join(WORKSPACE, rel)


def parent_of(rel: str) -> str:
    """Directory containing `rel`, as a workspace-relative path ('.' at the root)."""
    d = posixpath.dirname(rel.rstrip("/"))
    return d or "."


def basename_of(rel: str) -> str:
    return posixpath.basename(rel.rstrip("/"))
